Every word of law in this repository comes from these sources. Lexifina reads them and records each act's dated versions, and the laws that made each change, in one corpus file. This repository is built from that file.

The build made 988 commits:

| Commits | Kind |
| ---: | --- |
| 572 | first version of an act |
| 416 | one law on its in-force day |
| 0 | changes no amendment note explains, credited to the publisher |

Other counts:
- 1 commits on the branch `futuro`: laws not yet in force on 2026-11-02.
- 123 commits dated 1 January 1970: in force before 1970, with the real day in their `In-force` trailer.
- 820 laws in the record.
- 2310 tags.

## Dates

Each commit is dated at noon (America/Montevideo) on the day the change took effect. The other dates, enacted and published, are trailers.

When a law gives no date of its own, the default rule applies: Código Civil art. 1: laws are binding by their promulgation by the Poder Ejecutivo, and the promulgation is deemed known ten days after it is verified in the capital. A law's own vigencia clause overrides this. A constitutional reform approved by plebiscite takes effect when the Corte Electoral proclaims the result (Constitución, Disposición transitoria A). https://www.impo.com.uy/bases/codigo-civil/16603-1994/1

## How the history is built

`tools/build.py` turns the corpus into this history in one pass: one commit per law, on the day it took effect. The same corpus always gives the same commit hashes. `tools/check_repo.py` reads every version back from git and compares it with the corpus. `tools/on.py` shows the law as it stood on any date. The corpus format is described in `tools/SCHEMA.md`.

- Corpus: sha256 `b04a0ff51331827ed6dd1bdc9d301155e84133116da06994ea1a87858cf72825` (35699054 bytes).
- Builder blob hashes (`git hash-object`): `build.py` `a4faa25af85facb64b956b372f6b528aafdd7ec1`, `check.py` `3d00541d258745461ab52b1b0cb4e1716a27a95d`, `on.py` `644a6c0857fbcc72562032b58d57aa113fee0641`.
- Python 3.14.4; git version 2.53.0.

## Checks

1549 versions of 572 acts read back from git on the day each took effect and on the day before: 0 mismatches. Trailers: 0 commits whose trailers git cannot parse. Health: 0 merge commits on main, 0 commit dates that go backwards, 0 commits dated after the build.

A second build from the same corpus into an empty folder gave identical hashes for all 975 history commits and 2145 tags (history tip `2f329ad46c19154735d17dee22739e3326c05615`).

## Decisions made by hand

- Constitution: the dated texts are the Parlamento's editions of 1967, 1989, 1994, 1997 and 2004; today's text is IMPO's consolidated text. The units each reform changed come from comparing consecutive Parlamento editions word by word, and the build stops unless they equal the units IMPO's own notes ("redacción dada por", "agregado por") assign to that plebiscite.
- Constitution: the Parlamento and IMPO transcriptions differ in a few words in about 40 units (listed in verification.json). These are not changes in the law, so a unit uses IMPO's text from its last reform on (throughout, if no reform touched it), and the Parlamento's text only before that.
- Constitution: where a reform added incisos to an article (arts. 47, 67, 188), the earlier text is IMPO's text minus those incisos whenever that has exactly the Parlamento's earlier words.
- Constitution: the Parlamento's 2004 edition changes words in a few articles that no reform and no IMPO note explains (verification.json, parlamento_changes_no_reform_explains); they are not committed as changes.
- Constitution: the 1994, 1996 and 2004 reforms took effect when the Corte Electoral proclaimed the result. No source we read dates those proclamations, so their versions are dated on the plebiscite day, and each law's note says so.
- Constitution: the 1989 reform is dated 1 May 1990, the day its transitional provision V) puts it in force.
- Constitution: the Parlamento letters the 1994 and 2004 transitional provisions as a second V) and a further Z); IMPO letters them V') and Z"). IMPO's lettering is used (disp-trans-v-prima, disp-trans-z-segunda).
- Laws: IMPO gives only today's text, with notes naming the amending law and article for each change, and a link to the article as originally published. The first version of an amended article is that original text; today's text follows the last amending law. A text in between is used only when a source gives it: the wording the earlier amending law quotes (IMPO's original of that law), today's text without an item IMPO says a later law added, or the earlier text with an item replaced by the wording the amending law quotes for it. Otherwise the change is shown on the next known day, credited to every law in between, and verification.json lists it as unresolved.
- Laws: an amending law takes effect on the day its own vigencia article gives ("regirá a partir del …", read from IMPO's text of the article its notes point to, or for Ley 18.719 its art. 3, read by hand), else ten days after its publication in the Diario Oficial (Código Civil art. 1).
- Laws: amending laws that took effect before the norm's own first day are already in its text and are not credited.
- Código Civil: the oldest version is the text approved by Ley Nº 16.603 (promulgated 19 October 1994, published 21 November 1994, in force 1 December 1994 by Código Civil art. 1), the re-edition IMPO consolidates. The code of 1868 and its amendments before 1994 are not in IMPO's data, so the first commit is "oldest version available".
- Código Civil arts. 2393 to 2405: IMPO still prints them, with a note that Ley Nº 19.920 art. 62 repealed the Ley Nº 10.084 that added them; they are removed on Ley 19.920's day.
- Vigencia articles read by hand where IMPO's notes do not point to one: Ley 18.719 art. 3, Ley 17.703 art. 46, Ley 18.387 art. 255, Ley 19.075 art. 29, Ley 19.920 art. 63, Ley 19.670 art. 2, Ley 15.903 art. 2, Ley 17.897 art. 23 (in force on promulgation; not backdated, see the next decision).
- No backdating (coordinator decision, 2026-11-01): a law whose own vigencia article gives a day before its promulgation or its publication is dated ten days after its publication (Código Civil art. 1), the day every part gives it, with the day it states kept here: Ley 17.897 (art. 23: "desde su promulgación", 14 September 2005; published 19 September 2005) from 29 September 2005; Ley 18.387 (art. 255: ten days after its promulgation, 2 November 2008; published 3 November 2008) from 13 November 2008; Ley 18.719 (art. 3: 1 January 2011; published 5 January 2011) from 15 January 2011; Ley 20.446 (art. 3: 1 January 2026; published 8 January 2026) from 18 January 2026, its own version after Ley 20.443's of 1 January 2026.
- Código Penal: in force 1 August 1934 by Ley Nº 9.414 art. 2, which IMPO's note on the code cites. IMPO's text is the update of the Versión Oficial of 26 October 1967 (Decreto 698/967), and IMPO records no amendment before Ley 13.737 (1969); changes made between 1934 and 1969, if any, are not shown and would appear inside the next recorded change of that article. A sample of 11 unamended articles has exactly the words of IMPO's 1933 original text.
- Código Penal: Ley 19.138 takes effect ten days after its regulation by the Poder Ejecutivo, which we did not date; its one change (art. 350 BIS literal C) is dated by the default rule.
- Unit text: IMPO's hard-wrapped lines are joined into paragraphs (one paragraph or lettered item per line); ordinal marks, quotes and IMPO's "(*)" amended markers are normalised once.
- Batch 2 codes are rebuilt the same way as the Código Civil and Penal: the original text of each amended article from IMPO's TEXTO ORIGINAL link, today's text from IMPO, sourced intermediate texts only, and each amending law in force on the day its own vigencia article gives, else ten days after publication (Código Civil art. 1).
- Batch 2: IMPO notes "Derogado anteriormente por" and "Redacción derogada por" are read as events of that law; an article repealed and later given new wording is absent in between.
- Batch 2: IMPO scopes changes in the CGP and CPP to "ordinales" (e.g. 547.1); such a note is read as a scoped change to the article, never as a change to the whole article.
- Batch 2, anachronism rule: where a unit would show today's wording before the last law IMPO says gave it was in force, it reads "[La fuente no da el texto vigente en esta fecha.]" until that day (or is absent if that law inserted it); the units are listed below.
- CGP: in force 20 November 1989 by its own art. 547 (as worded by Ley 16.053, which took effect before the code and is therefore part of its first text). The first version is therefore the code as in force on that day (Ley 15.982 with Ley 16.053's changes), credited to the publisher as the oldest version available, naming both laws.
- CGP: vigencia articles read by hand: Ley 16.053 art. 3, 16.170 art. 2, 16.226 art. 2, 16.462 art. 2, 16.871 art. 101, 17.296 art. 2, 17.453 art. 51, 17.707 art. 28, 17.930 art. 2, 18.172 art. 2, 18.387 art. 255, 19.090 art. 2, 19.535 art. 2, 19.670 art. 2; a law whose stated day falls before its promulgation or publication (budget laws promulgated or published after the 1 January they name) is not backdated: it is dated ten days after its publication, and the day it states is in its day line below. Ley 19.090 ("el día hábil siguiente a los sesenta días de su promulgación", promulgated 14/06/2013) is dated 14/08/2013; Ley 20.130 (art. 6.1, first day of the third month after publication on 10/05/2023) 01/08/2023.
- CGP: an amending article's own clause ("El presente artículo entrará en vigencia a partir de la promulgación") overrides its law's day when it keeps the article's changes in order (post_pass article_vigencia).
- CGP: many amending laws quote a single ordinal ("ARTICULO 349.3", "Sustitúyese el artículo 29.1"); such a quote is not taken for the whole article, so that intermediate text is unknown and the change is shown on the next day with a known text, credited to every law in between.
- CGP, arts. 64 and 64 BIS: Ley 20.531 (Rendición de Cuentas 2025, promulgated 01/10/2026, in force 1 January 2027 by its art. 2) replaces both articles whole (its arts. 255 and 256). IMPO marks them ("ver vigencia") but has not consolidated them: today's text is IMPO's. The 1 January 2027 version (future branch) is built from Ley 20.531's own text on IMPO and is NOT checked against a consolidated text: each quotation without its "ARTÍCULO N.-" heading (art. 64 keeps the quoted epigraph "Forma de los actos procesales. Tecnologías de la información y la comunicación." as its first line), the full stop after the closing quotation ending the article.
- CGP, absent between: Artículo 452: absent from 2008-11-13 (Ley Nº 18.387, "derogado anteriormente") until 2013-08-14.
- CGP, added part removed: Artículo 548: before 1992-01-01, today's text without Apartado ordinal 1º) inciso 2º) (Ley Nº 16.226 art. 327 quotes it).
- Código de Comercio: the first version is the code of 1865 (Ley Nº 817) as IMPO gives it, dated 24 January 1866, the promulgation of Ley Nº 841, which fixed the day the code began to apply; that day is only in IMPO's scan of Ley Nº 841, which is not read (no OCR). The version is credited to the publisher as the oldest version available, naming Leyes 817 and 841.
- Código de Comercio: each amended article starts from IMPO's original 1865 text (textos-originales-ley/817-1865); unamended articles carry IMPO's consolidated text throughout. In a fixed sample of 12 unamended articles, 10 have the original's words exactly; arts. 66 ("hacerse,", "asiento,", "foja" for "hoja") and 382 ("los" for "lo") differ in punctuation or one word, with no amending law recorded, so no change is committed for them.
- Código de Comercio: vigencia articles read by hand: Decreto-Ley 14.701 art. 127 (1 November 1977), Ley 16.060 art. 511 (sixty days after publication on 01/11/1989: 31/12/1989), Ley 17.930 art. 2, Ley 18.387 art. 255; laws of 1878 and 1882 that IMPO gives no publication day for are dated ten days after promulgation.
- Código de Comercio, Libro IV (quiebras): IMPO keeps it as two entries, arts. 1523 a 1781 and 1764 a 1785, whose original texts are the 1865 arts. 1523-1747 and 1748-1768 (the numbering changed with the 1878 and 1916 texts). Ley Nº 1.422 replaced the whole book, in force 1 February 1879 by IMPO's summary of it, and Ley Nº 2.666 (1900) replaced it again; IMPO gives neither text, so from 1879 both units read the placeholder until Ley Nº 18.387 repealed them; the later partial changes IMPO lists inside that unknown text are not committed (listed under "whole rewrites" below).
- Batch 2: decree-laws of 1973-1985 (IMPO "Decreto Ley Nº") are kind "decree", with their own keys (decreto-ley-<n>) and IMPO paths (decretos-ley/).
- Código de Comercio, whole rewrites: art-1523-1781: the 1865 text until 1879-02-01, then "[La fuente no da el texto vigente en esta fecha.]" (Ley Nº 1.422 replaced it whole; IMPO's summary: "empiece a regir desde el 1º de Febrero de 1879"), removed on 2008-11-13; not credited within the unknown text: ley-5392, ley-5548, ley-8045, ley-8301, ley-8461, ley-9280, ley-13665; art-1764-1785: the 1865 text until 1879-02-01, then "[La fuente no da el texto vigente en esta fecha.]" (Ley Nº 1.422 replaced it whole; IMPO's summary: "empiece a regir desde el 1º de Febrero de 1879"), removed on 2008-11-13; not credited within the unknown text: ley-17292.
- CPP: in force 1 November 2017 by its art. 403 (as worded by Ley 19.510). Leyes 19.436, 19.474, 19.510, 19.511 and 19.544 (the last in force with the code, by its art. 11) changed it before that day, so the first version is the oldest version available; where a later law changed such an article, its first text is the wording the earlier law quotes (post_pass first_text_from_earlier_law), else the placeholder.
- CPP: arts. 383 to 392 (suspensión condicional del proceso) are absent from Ley 19.889's day until Ley 20.519 (IMPO note "Reincorporado por", in force ten days after its publication on 14/09/2026) puts them back: its art. 9 restores them as Título II of Libro VI. Ley 20.519 has no vigencia article (its art. 10 is about regulation), so that day is its own; batch 18 dates its own text on the same day.

- Batch 3 acts are rebuilt the same way as batch 2: each amended article starts from IMPO's original text, today's text is IMPO's consolidated text, intermediate texts are only those an amending law quotes or IMPO's notes determine, and each amending law is in force on the day its own vigencia article gives, else ten days after publication (Código Civil art. 1). Batch 2's readings of IMPO's notes and its anachronism rule (placeholder "[La fuente no da el texto vigente en esta fecha.]") apply.
- Batch 3: IMPO's original texts end with the next section's heading, in capitals ("PROCESO INFRACCIONAL ADUANERO") or as a short line with no closing punctuation ("De los consorcios"); such trailing lines are dropped from first texts and quoted wordings.
- Batch 3: articles are dated by each amending law's general in-force day as batch 2 does; where a law also amends an act of corpus.json or batch 2, it has the same day there.
- Batch 3: Ley Nº 19.889 (Ley de Urgente Consideración) was a candidate but is left out: IMPO's consolidated text of it gives 253 of its 476 articles only as "(*)" pointers to the laws they amended, so it has no self-standing consolidated text to rebuild; its changes are in those laws' histories.
- Ley 17.250: in force 27 August 2000, ten days after its publication on 17/08/2000 (it has no vigencia clause). Vigencia articles read by hand: Ley 18.046 art. 2 and Ley 18.362 art. 2 (1 January 2007 and 2009).
- Ley 17.250, arts. 16 and 42: Ley Nº 20.531 (Rendición de Cuentas 2025, promulgated 01/10/2026, in force 1 January 2027 by its art. 2) replaces art. 16 whole (its art. 108), and the acápite and literales C) and F) of art. 42 and adds its literal J) (art. 117). IMPO marks them ("ver vigencia") but has not consolidated them: today's text is IMPO's. The 1 January 2027 version (future branch) is built from Ley 20.531's own text on IMPO and is NOT checked against a consolidated text: art. 16 is the quoted article without its "ARTÍCULO 16.-" heading; in art. 42 the quoted acápite replaces the first paragraph, literal F) is the quoted literal with the six paragraphs under it (up to literal G), and literal J) follows literal I); the full stop after each closing quotation ends its paragraph.
- Ley 17.250, art. 31: Literal I) was added by Ley 19.149 and reworded by Ley 20.212; the 2014 wording is not determined by IMPO's notes, so the change is shown on 1 January 2024, credited to both laws.

- CAROU: in force 18 March 2015, 180 days after its promulgation on 19/09/2014 (its art. 276).
- CAROU: Leyes 19.322 and 19.394 changed no words: each suspended the vigencia of arts. 224, 225, 227 to 257 and 269 (and of art. 275 as to its repeal of procedural articles of Ley 13.318), until 1 March 2016 and then until 16 July 2017, retroactively (each validates proceedings under the old law from the earlier date). They are recorded as wording-unchanged commits on their in-force days (ten days after publication; neither has a vigencia clause), crediting the 35 articles whose IMPO notes name them, with a note on each law; the articles' text stays in the tree throughout. The day the suspension ended (16 July 2017) is not a commit.

- Ley 12.091 has no vigencia article; it is in force from 5 February 1954, ten days after its publication on 26 January 1954 (Código Civil art. 1).
- Ley 12.091, art. 1: Ley 14.106 art. 309 (1973) and Ley 16.736 art. 332 (1996) each added an inciso, and Ley 19.096 art. 1 (in force 12 July 2013, ten days after publication) added two more ("Agréganse ... los siguientes incisos"). Ley 19.535 art. 113 (in force 1 January 2018 by its art. 2) then added two incisos "como incisos quinto y sexto"; IMPO notes the first of them as the new wording of inciso 5, the second inciso Ley 19.096 had added ("El Poder Ejecutivo reglamentará las condiciones exigibles a las embarcaciones ..."), and its consolidated text no longer has that inciso, so that reading is followed and the change is credited to Ley 19.535. IMPO's consolidated text keeps only the last wording, so the 2013 wording is the 1996 text with the two incisos Ley 19.096 art. 1 quotes (IMPO's original text of that article) added at the end, word for word; it shows from 12 July 2013, credited to Ley 19.096, and the 2018 version is credited to Ley 19.535 only.
- Ley 12.091, art. 1 (incisos 2 and 3) and art. 23: the incisos Ley 14.106 art. 309 and Ley 16.736 art. 332 add and the 1959 wording Ley 12.613 art. 1 gives art. 23 are quotations that end with the closing quote before the sentence's full stop, so each ends with that full stop, as the later wordings do.
- Ley 12.091: every article was compared with IMPO's original text; apart from the articles a law changed (arts. 1, 11, 17 and 23), the differences are the chapter headings IMPO's original prints inside the articles.

Amending laws' days: Ley Nº 18.046 2007-01-01 (its vigencia article: "regirá a partir del 1º de enero de 2007" (artículo 2)); Ley Nº 20.446 2026-01-18 (published 2026-01-08; Código Civil art. 1: ten days after publication (not backdated: its vigencia article: "regirá a partir del 1° de enero de 2026" gives 2026-01-01, before its publication on 2026-01-08) (artículo 3)).
- Ley 17.968: of a fixed sample of 2 articles IMPO records no change to, 1 have the words of IMPO's original text; art. 4 differ only by a correction, a paragraph break or a trailing heading no amending law explains, and read as IMPO gives them today from the first version (not committed as changes).
- Ley 17.968 has no vigencia article; it is in force from 15 June 2006, ten days after its publication on 5 June 2006 (Código Civil art. 1).
- Ley 17.968, art. 1: Ley 18.046 art. 133 (in force 1 January 2007 by its art. 2) and Ley 20.446 art. 362 replaced the whole article. IMPO's consolidated text keeps only the last wording, so the 2007 wording is the passage Ley 18.046 art. 133 quotes (IMPO's original text of that article), word for word; IMPO's copy of that quotation breaks lines inside sentences and leaves out the final full stop, so its paragraphs are rejoined as the quotation's layout gives them and it ends with the full stop (only line breaks and that stop differ). It shows from 1 January 2007, credited to Ley 18.046; the 2026 version is credited to Ley 20.446 only.
- Ley 17.968, art. 1: Ley 20.446 says in its art. 3 that it governs from 1 January 2026, before its publication on 8 January 2026; no law is backdated in this history, so its change shows from 18 January 2026, ten days after publication, as in every other part.
- Ley 17.968, art. 3: Ley 18.046 art. 134 (1 January 2007) replaced its inciso segundo; the first wording is IMPO's original text.
- Ley 17.968, art. 4: IMPO's original text ends "efectivizarlo." followed by the closing formula, where its consolidated text ends "efectivizarlo.-"; IMPO records no change, so the consolidated text is shown from the first version.
- Ley 17.968: every article was compared with IMPO's original text; apart from the articles a law changed (arts. 1 and 3) and art. 4 (above), the articles have the same words.
- Ley 17.968, relaid: art-001: Ley Nº 18.046 art. 133's wording re-read from its quotation's layout, with its full stop (1 versions).
- Batch 18 acts are rebuilt the same way as batches 2 to 17, with their readings: each amended article starts from IMPO's original text, today's text is IMPO's consolidated text, intermediate texts are only those an amending law quotes or IMPO's notes determine, and each amending law is in force on the day its own vigencia article gives, else ten days after publication (Código Civil art. 1); a law shared with an earlier part has the same day there.
- Ley 19.480: in force 2017-01-27 (published 2017-01-17; Código Civil art. 1: ten days after publication). Amending laws' days: Ley Nº 19.924 2021-01-01 (its vigencia article: "regirá a partir del 1° de enero de 2021" (artículo 3)); Ley Nº 19.996 2022-01-01 (its vigencia article: "regirá a partir del 1° de enero de 2022" (artículo 2)).
- Ley 19.480: of a fixed sample of 5 articles IMPO records no change to, 4 have the words of IMPO's original text; art. 6 differ only by a correction, a paragraph break or a trailing heading no amending law explains, and read as IMPO gives them today from the first version (not committed as changes).
- Ley 19.480 has no vigencia article; it is in force from 27 January 2017, ten days after its publication on 17 January 2017 (Código Civil art. 1).
- Ley 19.480, art. 5: Ley 19.924 art. 749 (in force 1 January 2021 by its art. 3) and Ley 19.996 art. 357 (1 January 2022, by its art. 2) each replaced the whole article. IMPO's consolidated text keeps only the last wording, so the 2021 wording is the passage Ley 19.924 art. 749 quotes (IMPO's original text of that article); that quotation ends with the closing quote before the sentence's full stop, so the wording ends with that full stop, as the 2022 wording does. The first wording is IMPO's original text, which reads "e! literal B)" in literal D), a misprint shown as IMPO gives it until the 2021 wording replaces it.
- Ley 19.480, art. 7: added by Ley 19.924 art. 750 (1 January 2021) and replaced by Ley 19.996 art. 358 (1 January 2022). The 2021 wording is the passage Ley 19.924 art. 750 quotes (IMPO's original text of that article), ending with the full stop after its closing quote, as for art. 5; the article is absent before 2021.
- Ley 19.480: every article was compared with IMPO's original text; apart from the articles a law changed (arts. 5 and 7), the difference is the closing formula.
- Ley 19.480, quote end restored: art-005: Ley Nº 19.924 art. 749's wording ends with its closing quote and full stop (1 versions); art-007: Ley Nº 19.924 art. 750's wording ends with its closing quote and full stop (1 versions).
- Ley 19.061: in force 2013-02-01 (published 2013-01-22; Código Civil art. 1: ten days after publication). Amending laws' days: Ley Nº 19.824 2019-10-05 (published 2019-09-25; Código Civil art. 1: ten days after publication); Ley Nº 19.996 2022-01-01 (its vigencia article: "regirá a partir del 1° de enero de 2022" (artículo 2)).
- Ley 19.061: of a fixed sample of 12 articles IMPO records no change to, 12 have the words of IMPO's original text.
- Ley 19.061 has no vigencia article; it is in force from 1 February 2013, ten days after its publication on 22 January 2013 (Código Civil art. 1). Its art. 10 sets equipment duties "a partir de los ciento ochenta días de la promulgación"; that term changes no words, so no version follows from it.
- Ley 19.061, art. 14: replaced by Ley 19.824 art. 51 (in force 5 October 2019, ten days after its publication on 25 September 2019; IMPO's note gives no vigencia article for it).
- Ley 19.061, arts. 5 and 10: replaced by Ley 19.996 arts. 41 and 42 (in force 1 January 2022 by its art. 2); the first wording of each is IMPO's original text.
- Ley 19.061: every article was compared with IMPO's original text; apart from the articles a law changed (arts. 5, 10 and 14), the differences are the chapter headings IMPO's original prints inside the articles and the closing formula.
- Ley 18.126: in force 2007-06-01 (published 2007-05-22; Código Civil art. 1: ten days after publication). Amending laws' days: Ley Nº 20.212 2024-01-01 (its vigencia article: "regirá a partir del 1° de enero de 2024" (artículo 2)); Ley Nº 20.446 2026-01-18 (published 2026-01-08; Código Civil art. 1: ten days after publication (not backdated: its vigencia article: "regirá a partir del 1° de enero de 2026" gives 2026-01-01, before its publication on 2026-01-08) (artículo 3)).
- Ley 18.126: of a fixed sample of 11 articles IMPO records no change to, 10 have the words of IMPO's original text; art. 13 differ only by a correction, a paragraph break or a trailing heading no amending law explains, and read as IMPO gives them today from the first version (not committed as changes).
- Ley 18.126 has no vigencia article; it is in force from 1 June 2007, ten days after its publication on 22 May 2007 (Código Civil art. 1).
- Ley 18.126, art. 3: Ley 20.212 art. 234 (in force 1 January 2024 by its art. 2) added literal H), and Ley 20.446 art. 240 gave literal D) new wording. IMPO's consolidated text keeps only the last wording, so the 2024 wording is IMPO's original text of art. 3 with the literal Ley 20.212 art. 234 quotes (IMPO's original text of that article) added at the end, word for word; it shows from 1 January 2024, credited to Ley 20.212, and the 2026 version is credited to Ley 20.446 only.
- Ley 18.126, art. 3: Ley 20.446 says in its art. 3 that it governs from 1 January 2026, before its publication on 8 January 2026; no law is backdated in this history, so its change shows from 18 January 2026, ten days after publication, as in every other part.
- Ley 18.126, art. 11: Ley 20.212 art. 235 (1 January 2024) replaced the whole article; its new wording has no heading and no opening inciso ("(Creación e integración).- Créanse las Mesas de Desarrollo Rural."), so from 2024 the article is IMPO's text today, without them.
- Ley 18.126, art. 13: IMPO's original text ends "ley." followed by the closing formula, where its consolidated text ends "ley.-"; IMPO records no change, so the consolidated text is shown from the first version.
- Ley 18.126: every article was compared with IMPO's original text; apart from the articles a law changed (arts. 3 and 11) and art. 13 (above), the differences are the chapter headings IMPO's original prints inside the articles.
- Ley 18.126, replayed in between: art-003: Ley Nº 20.212 art. 234's own words (add_item) applied from 2024-01-01, until the next law replaces it.
- Current-text acts (breadth first): each of these laws has one version, IMPO's consolidated text as published today, credited to IMPO as the oldest version available (first_law null). Its day is IMPO's own last-change day: the latest date IMPO's notes give for a law that changed the text (the date IMPO prints for that law, its promulgation), or the law's own promulgation date when the notes name none. Articles IMPO marks repealed whole, bare "(*)" articles of amendments and articles IMPO prints without text are not included. A law IMPO marks repealed whole ends with a version without provisions, credited to the repealing law on its in-force day. Titles are IMPO's own names of the laws, as IMPO prints them.

- Current-text acts (breadth first): each of these laws has one version, IMPO's consolidated text as published today, credited to IMPO as the oldest version available (first_law null). Its day is IMPO's own last-change day: the latest date IMPO's notes give for a law that changed the text (the date IMPO prints for that law, its promulgation), or the law's own promulgation date when the notes name none. Articles IMPO marks repealed whole, bare "(*)" articles of amendments and articles IMPO prints without text are not included. A law IMPO marks repealed whole ends with a version without provisions, credited to the repealing law on its in-force day. Titles are IMPO's own names of the laws, as IMPO prints them.

## What AI did and did not do

The scripts that read the sources and build this repository were written by Claude Code running Claude Opus 5.5 (`claude-opus-5-5`) under Lexifina's direction.

No model wrote, translated or corrected legal text. No model runs at build time. Every word of law in this repository comes from the official sources above.

The commit messages are written by `build.py` from the source data.

Model use at data-build time:
- A model (Claude) wrote Lexifina's code and chose the sources. It wrote no legal text: every unit is parsed from IMPO or Parlamento bytes.
- A model (Claude) wrote Lexifina's code and read the vigencia articles listed in decisions. It wrote no legal text: every unit is parsed from IMPO bytes, apart from the fixed placeholder sentence of the anachronism rule.
